GAZBOT V5 · Shadow Desk

The Shadow Desk — What’s Working, What’s Bleeding, What To Do Monday

Week of Mon 6 -> Fri 10 Jul 2026 · every one of the 35 shadow sims on the honest tick-repriced fills, adversarially verified

§1 The shadow desk this week — read this first

You told me to point the whole report at the 32 shadow sims, in plain English, and to cover the things the first cut missed — where the week's big runs were and whether we showed up, the footprints, the exit change we made on Wednesday, and how we'd filter the live gates so they stop chasing knives. This is that report. Here's the shape of it before you dive in.

The one thing to take away
The shadow desk splits cleanly down the middle, and the line is momentum vs fading. Eight sims made money on honest fills (+$2,440); the rest bled. Every winner rides a move; every big loser fades one, sits in dead tape, or rests a limit order and gets picked off. And the sharpest version of that — momentum done with confirmation makes +$2,344 while "ride any strength" loses −$1,017 — is the one finding an independent skeptic re-ran and it held. So "how do we make money Monday" has a plain answer: feed the confirmed thrust, starve the fade.

§1.1 Where the money was — and did we show up?

This time I used the full run census, not a top-8 (you were right to call that out). Across the seven contracts there were 540 big runs this week, and we sat out 413 of them. But here's the honest surprise when you look at all of them: the runs themselves aren't the leak. The with-the-move momentum sims that did show up already print +$2,441 on honest fills — while the fade book gave back −$11,040 fighting the very same moves. The money isn't in the runs we missed; it's in the fades we should never have taken. And most runs have no readable tell before they fire — the tape and even the L2 book stay flat or point the wrong way until the break — so the play is to join a move once it's clearly moving, not predict it. The one clean, repeatable exception across contracts is the US cash-open window (13:00–15:00 UTC), where half the week's biggest bursts live.

§1.2 So how do we grab them from Monday?

The capture plan — the point of the whole report
Don't build a new edge. Point the one proven entry we already have — confirmed-momentum + chandelier (`tw_mnq_thrust_cont`, +$992) — at the FLOW-LED continuation runs we keep sitting out on MNQ and MES (price already leaning with a positive VWAP slope, ATR expanding — that's the one cluster with a clean pre-run signature). In the same breath, stop the reversion sims fading into the vacuum runs — that's where the −$11,040 went. For the US-open bursts on M2K/MYM/MBT, shadow a simple opening-range break (never pre-position — the open whips both ways). And the no-tape contracts (MCL, and MBT's book) are a data-capture job, not tradeable yet. All shadow-first; arm nothing with size until it proves a second week.
One rule that must not get lost — momentum is TWO-SIDED
Every momentum gate trades both ways: it longs a rising thrust and shorts a falling one. Never turn a momentum gate long-only. This week the momentum short side (+$1,435) actually beat its long side (+$1,006) — on MES and MGC the short side was the only green side — so a long-only version would have binned the bigger half of the edge. The thing we kill is FADING (selling rips / buying dips against the move), never shorting; the "shorts bled" this week were the fade book's shorts fighting up-moves, a completely different behaviour. (Prerequisite for ever taking momentum live: re-enable shorts on the desk.)

§1.3 The exit change, before and after

On Wednesday afternoon we threw out the nine-mechanism exit stack for two simple ones — reversion gets a 1.5-ATR stop and a 2R target, momentum gets a 1-ATR stop and a single trailing chandelier. The honest read after the switch: momentum keeps every dollar on that one chandelier — it matches the old eight-mechanism spine for free (62% of trades close at about +$10). Reversion still bleeds, and the logged worry is confirmed — the 2R fade target only fires on about one trade in ten; a nearer target trims the bleed but never fixes it, because the fade's problem is the entries, not the exit. Simpler exits are as good or better; the work is on the entry side.

§1.4 The live gates, and the knife-filter

Both live gates (`vwap_dip`, `vwap_pullback`) are mean-reversion, and their whole shadow brood bled −$5,157 this week. Here's the counter-intuitive bit worth your attention: the "only fade in quiet tape" filter is backwards — the book bled worst in the quietest tape. The fix isn't a calm-tape band, it's a direction/trend veto — stop fading into a live move. On the knife-filter you asked about: I owe you the honest result — aggressor-tape confirmation on entry is null (p=0.40), and the flow-flip exit that looked great at one-minute dies at tick resolution. The obvious bolt-on doesn't pay. The one use still open is L2 absorption to gate the knife-catching fades — but that needs another week or two of depth capture before it's even testable.

§1.5 What survived the skeptic

Every bankable claim got re-run by an independent skeptic. Most clever per-sim tweaks fell — on one week a filter is usually just "trade less" in disguise. What held was the big structural stuff: the confirmed-vs-loose momentum split (the skeptic reproduced +$1,828), and that the worst faders genuinely bleed and should be stood down, not tuned. Read the amber rows below as leads for the shadow book, not instructions.

§1.6 So what do we do Monday?

The plan, in order
1. Lean the book toward confirmed momentum on the equity/gold micros, led by MNQ — and only when there's a real push and real amplitude already in the tape (the one pocket that was green all five days). 2. Stand down the confirmed bleeders — passive_chop first (it has no favourable pocket anywhere), then the fade family; and never let the desk fade a live move. 3. Bin the "ride any strength" family (tw_ride_strength, tw_mcl_short_with_strength) — it's the −$1,017 drag. 4. Everything else stays shadow-only for another week; arm nothing with size until it survives a second, independent week. Feed the confirmed thrust, starve the fade — slowly, on what proves itself twice.

§1.7 How to read the rest

First, where the money was — the full run census on each contract (every big run, not a top-8), whether we showed up, and the footprint before each. Then the through-lines, opening with "how we grab the runs from Monday" (the capture plan across all contracts), then momentum confirmed-vs-loose, the exit before/after, the live gates and the knife-filter, the L2 order book — our first honest look (the data you caught me leaving out), the OFF switch, greenfield, the sizing ladder, and "your leads this week" — every mid-week musing walked to its verdict. Then the winners, one plain dossier each (lean into these), and the bleeders (fix or retire these). Each sim dossier carries a coloured verdict at the top — green works, amber means one filter might fix it, red means retire. The green verdicts, the confirmed-momentum finding, the OFF-switch, and the capture plan are what to act on.

Chapter

Where The Money Was — The Week&rsquo;s Big Runs, And Did We Show Up

⚠ A skeptic re-ran the money claim — it FELL (read as a lead, not a rule)
REPRODUCES but does not survive as a bankable edge. Every descriptive fact checks out on honest cost-inclusive real_pnl: 76 runs at 1.5xATR, 17 caught for +$993.5 real, fought ~+$18, and thrust_cont +$992 / momentum +$495 / orb +$318 / ride_strength SHORT -$527 all confirmed — so it is NOT curve-fit and NOT cost-blind. BUT it fails as bankable money on three counts. (1) Cherry-picked winning subset: the +$993 is gross winnings of run-aligned trades pulled out of a book that LOST money this week — honest MNQ real_pnl was -$353 (report window, 458 trades) to -$458 (full 7d, 467 trades); you cannot bank the +993 without the fades that gave it all back. (2) Thin-n: top 2 runs = 54% and top 4 = 81% of the $993; the 'FLOW-LED already prints' claim rests on 4 caught runs = +$438 of which 2 trades carry +$432. (3) The actionable FLOW-LED continuation is an unproven shadow-first hypothesis — the report's own honest-null shows the pre-run tape does not forecast direction (8/19), leaving only a fragile +0.19-ATR VWAP slope on n=19. Real insight that survives: the run-catchers are genuinely +EV and the fades bleed. Real number: the honest MNQ book this week was a LOSS of about GBP 278 (-$353.5 report window; -GBP 361 on the full 7 days), not the ~GBP 150/wk implied.

§2 The biggest runs on MNQ this week — did we show up?

MNQ printed 76 runs of 1.5×ATR or bigger in the last seven days (a 15-minute move of at least 73.9 points, against a typical 15-min range of 49.2). That is not a top-eight story — it is the whole tape. We showed up for 17 of them, we were positioned against 10, and we sat out the other 49. When we did align with a run we banked +$993 of honest money; the shapes that print it are already in the book. The bleed is not the runs — it is the fading we do in the quiet between them.

§2.2 The four clusters, and how blunt the participation is

The blunt version: on the 36 VACUUM snapbacks we fought seven of them — we were fading into the move as it detonated. On the 19 FLOW-LED continuations we fought zero and simply watched 15 of them go by. That asymmetry is the whole chapter: we get run over by reversals and we no-show the continuations.

§2.3 What the ten minutes before each cluster looked like

Averaging the signal funnel over the 600 seconds before every run, by cluster:

Clusterrvolatr%vwap dist%vwap slope (ATR)Read
VACUUM0.580.064−0.05−0.53price drifting the wrong way first — a snapback
FLOW-LED0.610.061+0.00+0.19price already leaning the run's way
OPEN/NEWS0.790.098−0.01−0.00biggest amplitude, no directional lean

Two honest things fall out. First, rvol is below 1.0 in every cluster (~0.6) — these runs ignite out of quiet, so relative volume is not the trigger you'd hope for. Second, the only cluster with a clean, same-direction pre-run signature is FLOW-LED: a positive VWAP slope (+0.19 ATR) with price sitting on the run side of VWAP. That is a plain-English "it's already going, and it keeps going." VACUUM is the mirror — price leaning the opposite way (slope −0.53) right before it reverses, which is exactly why fading it is a trap.

THE TAPE DOES NOT LEAD — AN HONEST NULL

I wanted the aggressor tape to forecast these. It doesn't. Signing the 5-minute pre-run aggressor volume toward the direction the run eventually went: FLOW-LED leaned the right way in only 8 of 19 runs (median −43 contracts, i.e. slightly against); OPEN/NEWS in only 5 of 18 (median −971, strongly against — the news flips whoever was leaning); VACUUM 14 of 36. The tape that "drives" a FLOW-LED run is concurrent, not a heads-up. So the pre-run edge, such as it is, lives in the price slope, not the tape.

§2.4 The L2 book (MNQ, Thu 07-09 14:17 → Fri 07-10 21:00 UTC)

Ten MNQ runs fall inside the depth window. I pulled the top-5 book in 30-second buckets across the two minutes before each and watched the far side — the side price has to eat to run. The result is a clean negative:

RunFar-side top-5 (2min→0)ImbalanceTell?
07-10 15:27 DN37 → 42 → 40+0.02 to +0.03none — bid side grew
07-10 15:45 UP (FLOW-LED)38 → 37 → 38−0.05 to +0.05none — asks flat
07-09 14:21 UP (caught)35 → 33 → 31−0.02 to +0.05none material
07-10 15:05 UP (VACUUM)34 → 37 → 35−0.03 flatnone

Across every run in the window the top-5 sat at a stable 33–42 contracts a side with imbalance pinned near zero (±0.06), and the far side did not thin out ahead of the move, nor did imbalance lean the way the run went. The vacuum happens inside the run — levels are replaced faster than they're worth showing at 250ms — not visibly before it. MNQ's top-5 is simply too shallow and too fast to telegraph at this granularity. No repeatable L2 tell to flag for next week, with one honest caveat: we only capture five levels; a real depletion could be sitting deeper than we can see.

§2.5 The honest money left on the table

Full-fantasy ceiling — every one of the 76 runs, whole move, one lot — is $16,673. That is hindsight and nobody catches it. The grounded numbers:

RunsCeiling $Honest real $
Caught (aligned)173,519+993
Fought (opposed)102,664+15
Sat out4910,4890

When we aligned we converted +$993 of a $3,519 ceiling — about 28%, which is a fair haul for a 1-ATR-stop shape. Fighting the runs netted a near-wash (+$15) only because the exit spine cut the fades fast; the entries were wrong, the exits saved us. And the whole MNQ shadow book this week was −$353 real across 491 trades (versus a rosy +$2,492 optimistic) — so the runs made money and the chop gave it all back and more. Where did the chop bleed go? passive_chop −$806 and tw_ride_strength SHORT −$527 — fades. Where did the run money come from? tw_mnq_thrust_cont +$991, momentum_shadow +$495, orb +$318. We already own the run-catching machine.

§2.6 How we grab more of them from Monday

FLOW-LED continuation — the one clean sit-out. SHADOW-FIRST
This is the highest-value hypothesis and we already have the exact tool. The pre-run signature is plain: VWAP slope leaning the run direction (≥ about +0.15 ATR) with price on the run side of VWAP and ATR expanding. The trigger: on a fresh thrust that extends a further ~0.5 ATR in the slope direction, go with it. The exit is not new to build — it is tw_mnq_thrust_cont, whose money this week comes almost entirely from its RATCHET exits (LONG +$386 on 6 trades, SHORT +$352 on 4, holds ~12–17 minutes — exactly a run's length). The ask is simply to widen that shadow's arming so it fires on more of the 15 FLOW-LED runs we watched go by, and observe whether the extra fills hold their +28% conversion. Shadow-first, not arm-now — loosening a trigger always drags in false thrusts, and we measure that before it touches size.

OPEN/NEWS — biggest size, but a coin-flip to pre-position. SHADOW-FIRST
These 18 runs hold the fattest ceiling ($4,936) but the direction is genuinely unforecastable from what we capture — the pre-run tape leaned the wrong way in 13 of 18. So the honest play is not a prediction, it is a reaction: after the 13:30 UTC data / cash open, take the first 1-ATR thrust in its own direction with the same ratchet exit — a break-and-go. The missing piece is an event-clock gate (a "we are in the 13:30 window" flag) which we don't cleanly have in the funnel; name that as the data to add. Shadow-first.

VACUUM — not a new entry, a veto. STOP FADING
The 36 snapbacks move against the prior lean by construction — unforecastable in direction from what we capture. The win here is defensive: we fought seven of them, and the passive_chop / ride-strength-short fades that bled −$806 this week are largely these. Don't build to catch VACUUM; build the slope-veto that stops us fading into it (this ties straight to the reversion autopsy). No new arm.

MNQ TAKEAWAY

The money this week was in the runs (+$993 when we aligned) and the bleed was fading between them (−$806 chop). We showed up for 17 of 76 and banked about a quarter of what we caught. The single clean, repeatable pre-run signature we keep no-showing is FLOW-LED continuation — price already leaning, ATR expanding — and we already own the shape that prints it (thrust_cont + ratchet). So the move is widen that shadow, don't build new. The L2 top-5 book does not telegraph direction on MNQ, the aggressor tape does not lead, and rvol is quiet before runs — the only honest heads-up is the VWAP slope. OPEN/NEWS is the fattest prize but a coin-flip to pre-position: react to the first thrust, never predict it.

We fired into 10 of them and sat out the other 53, including the four biggest and the entire Thursday–Friday stretch where we can actually see the order book. When we did show up, the honest money was a shade positive (+$65.70 across 17 sim entries). Meanwhile the whole MES sim book bled −$1,044 real fighting the runs it stood in front of. This chapter walks every one of the 63, then asks the owner's question: which of these could we have grabbed, and how do we grab them from Monday.

Blunt version: 10 of 63 runs had a sim fire in the right direction inside ±5 minutes. And those 10 are almost all Monday–Tuesday. From Wednesday morning's −49-point flush onward, we sat out essentially everything — 40-plus runs in a row, including the four biggest of the week (the −49.2, −31.5, +27.5 and −26.2). The runs weren't small or rare; we just weren't in the market with a shape pointed the right way.

THE BOOK WE'RE MEASURED ON

Zoom out from the runs to the whole MES sim book this week: 404 filled trades, −$1,044.48 real — against an optimistic sim print of +$1,190, so the simulator is flattering MES by about $2.2k. Only two shapes finished green, and both are momentum-continuation: momentum_shadow +$102.61 (47 trades, 19 wins) and tw_mes_thrust_cont +$49.13 (26 trades, 12 wins). The bleeders are all fade/chop shapes standing in front of these runs: tw_ride_strength −$287, passive_chop −$183, dip_loose −$134. So the desk's MES loss this week is the story of these runs — we bled fading them and only banked when we rode them.

§3.4 3 · The footprint — what the 10 minutes before each cluster looked like

For 62 of the 63 runs there's a live funnel row in the 10 minutes before the move, so I could average the pre-run signature per cluster (atr_pct, rvol, VWAP distance, order-flow imbalance):

Clusteratr_pctrvolVWAP distOFIPlain read of the run-up
VACUUM0.0370.60−0.01−24quiet, below-average volume coil sitting on VWAP, then snaps
OPEN/NEWS0.0500.83−0.02−38hottest atr going in — the market already knows a print is coming
FLOW-LED0.0370.56+0.02−45compressed and quiet, then a one-sided aggressor burst lights it
UNCLASS0.0320.70−0.02+8the quietest, most featureless — genuine noise

The uncomfortable truth is that all four clusters come out of an rvol below 1.0 — below-average volume. These runs erupt from compression, not from a visible build-up. That's a real signature, but it's directionless: quiet coils are everywhere and most of them don't go anywhere. There's no clean "rvol was building for ten minutes" tell that separates a coil that fires from a coil that dozes.

So I pushed on the one cluster that both pays when caught and has an aggressor tape to look at — FLOW-LED. Do the aggressors that "drive" it show up before the run, or only during it? I summed net aggressor contracts in the two minutes before all ten FLOW-LED runs (tape covers 07-05 on):

THE HARD FINDING

The pre-run tape agreed with the run's eventual direction in only 4 of 10 FLOW-LED runs — worse than a coin flip. The 07-10 15:01 up run had −789 net (heavy selling) in the two minutes before it; the 15:27 down run had +1,399 net (heavy buying) before it. In other words the aggressor flow that "led" these runs is coincident with the run itself — the flow is the run. It is not a two-minutes-early warning you could act on. The scorecard's "FLOW-LED" label describes the cause once it's happening, not a forecastable set-up.

Which cluster do we sit out with a genuinely clean pre-run signature? Honestly — none of them, on the entry side. What's clean is the after-the-fact pattern: the only money we made came from being already long/short in the direction of an aggressor burst. That points the new-entry work at a reactive trigger, not a predictive one. More on that in section 6.

§3.5 4 · The L2 order book — is the vacuum visible before it opens?

Twelve census runs fall inside the depth capture window (Thu 07-09 14:17 → Fri 07-10 21:00 UTC, ~197k MES snapshots at ~250ms). All twelve were sit-outs, so this is exactly the set the owner wants a book read on. The question: in the 1–2 minutes before each run, did the far side of the book thin out / vanish (a vacuum you can see coming), and did imbalance lean the way the run went?

I measured mean book imbalance in the −90s to −30s window before each run and asked whether it leaned the run's direction:

THE L2 VERDICT — THIN AND NOISY THIS WEEK

Pre-run book lean matched the run direction in only 3 of 9 decisive runs (three were flat) — again worse than a coin flip. Two concrete examples with real sizes:

07-10 15:27, −18.2 down (FLOW): at −20s the book was 221 bid vs 394 ask across five levels (imbalance −0.28) — sellers genuinely stacked, and price fell. But the −90s→−30s window it grew out of was actually bid-lean (+0.055). The "tell" only appears in the final 20 seconds, i.e. coincident with the break, not ahead of it.

07-10 15:45, +14.8 up (VACUUM): the book was ask-heavy going in (309 ask vs 226 bid, imbalance −0.16) and price rose into it — the displayed asks got lifted/absorbed rather than holding. The book pointed the wrong way. That's the literal definition of the VACUUM cluster: displayed liquidity that lies.

So the clear answer for the owner: there is no clean, repeatable L2 lead on MES this week. Two days of top-5 depth at 250ms is too noisy, and where there is a signal it's coincident with the break or outright contrarian. I would not arm anything off book imbalance yet. The one shape worth watching next week: last-20-second far-side stacking on down-flushes was directionally right in the couple I hand-checked — but that's a coincident confirmation, not a predictor, and needs a fuller window before it's more than a hunch.

§3.6 5 · The honest money left on the table

Add up the directional range of all 63 runs and it's 962 points — a hindsight ceiling of about $4,811 on a single contract if you'd nailed every start and sold every extreme. That number is a fantasy and I want to be plain about why: most of it is either the directionless VACUUM coil (24 runs, 22 sat out) that you can't forecast, or OPEN/NEWS whipsaws like the 14:33 monster (a −24,193-contract sell wave that flushed then V-reversed +17) that chew up anything mechanical.

The capturable slice is much smaller and we already know its shape: the reactive momentum book. momentum_shadow and tw_mes_thrust_cont between them netted +$152 real this week riding exactly these bursts, and the runs we participated in netted +$65.70. Realistically the extra we left on the table is a few hundred dollars of FLOW-LED and post-flush continuation — not the $4.8k headline. The gap isn't "we missed a fortune," it's "we only had a shape pointed the right way one day in five."

§3.7 6 · How we grab them from Monday

The evidence says stop trying to predict these and get better at reacting to them. Here's the concrete plan per cluster.

§3.8 SHADOW-FIRST FLOW-LED & the momentum burst — widen what already works

Why: it's the only cluster that paid every time we caught it (+$174 across three), and the two green MES shapes already live here. Crucially, the winners fire on elevated rvol — momentum_shadow entered at mean rvol 1.23 and thrust_cont at 1.85, both with OFI around +59. They wait for volume to already be firing. That's exactly right, and it's why they're green while the fade shapes bleed.

The trigger: when rvol crosses roughly ≥1.2 and a 30–60s net-aggressor burst in trade_tick exceeds a size threshold in the break direction and price breaks the prior 5-second range — go with the break. Direction = the sign of the aggressor burst. Exit: the chandelier/thrust exit already wired to tw_mes_thrust_cont — no new exit to build.

Action: extend the existing thrust trigger to fire on more of the FLOW-LED and post-flush continuation runs we sat out (7 of 10 FLOW-LED missed). Keep it shadow — both shapes are green but on ~30–50 trades of one in-sample week, nowhere near the promotion-ladder gate. Watch it two more weeks across a trend regime before it goes near live-1.

§3.9 DON'T CHASE VACUUM — unforecastable from what we capture

24 runs, the biggest single cluster, and both the tape (moves against it by definition) and the top-5 book (3/9 lean agreement) point the wrong way beforehand. This is a real edge for someone, but not with what we record. To have any shot you'd need full-depth book beyond level 5 plus resting-order cancellation / iceberg detection — the rate at which displayed size is pulled just before the break — which 250ms top-5 snapshots don't show. Until we capture that, the honest move is to stop fading into these (kill the passive_chop / ride_strength fades that lost −$470 combined here), not to try to catch them.

§3.10 REACT, DON'T PREDICT OPEN / NEWS — the spike isn't tradeable, the second leg might be

22 runs, hottest pre-run atr (0.050), and the cluster where our participation actually lost money (we caught the 13:19/13:36 spikes late for −$77). Predicting the first move off a scheduled print is a coin-flip and the 14:33 reversal proves the danger. But note tw_mes_block_us_open, which stands aside through the open, still lost −$113 — so blindly blocking isn't free either. The tractable version: don't touch the first thrust bar; let the open print, then apply the same reactive momentum trigger to the continuation once rvol and flow agree. Shadow-only, same trigger as FLOW-LED above.

PLAIN TAKEAWAY — MES

MES gave us 63 real runs and roughly $4.8k of hindsight range this week; we showed up for 10 and the sim book still lost −$1,044 fading the other 53. Neither the aggressor tape nor the level-5 order book forecasts these one week in — the flow and the vacuum are both coincident with the break, not ahead of it. The only thing that paid is being already positioned in the direction of a live thrust, which is exactly what the two green shadows (momentum_shadow, tw_mes_thrust_cont, +$152 together) already do. So the Monday move is not to build a predictor — it's to widen the reactive thrust trigger onto the FLOW-LED and post-open-continuation runs we keep sitting out, kill the fades that bleed in front of them, and leave the VACUUM cluster alone until we capture deeper book. Trade less, react faster.

§4 The biggest runs on MGC this week — did we show up?

Gold gave us 57 real runs this week — 15-minute moves of at least 11.1 points ($110 a contract), 1.5× MGC's typical quarter-hour range of 7.4pt. We were pointed the right way on 24 of them, sat on our hands for 33, and of those sat-out ones we actively fought 8 — fired a fade straight into the move and got run over. On the sims that were aligned, the honest money during runs was +$756; on the sims caught leaning the wrong way it was −$273. The hindsight ceiling of all this run-money is about $9,060 a contract, of which we let $5,390 walk past untouched.

THE ONE-LINE READ

MGC runs are mostly VACUUM — price ripping the opposite way to the tape, which means the tape can't tell us the direction in advance, and this week the L2 book was too thin to see the vacuum coming either. The one clean, repeatable signature we keep sitting out is the US-open / data window (13:00–15:00 UTC) volatility burst. That's the shadow-first hypothesis. The bigger immediate win is defensive: stop letting the fade sims walk into these things.

§4.2 2 · Did we show up? Blunt version

24 caught · 33 sat out · 8 of those fought. On the 30 aligned sim-fills that sat inside a run, the honest money was +$756.40. On the 18 fills that were pointed the wrong way — a fade, or a mis-fired momentum short, walking into the move — it was −$273.16. So during the runs themselves the shadow book netted about +$483, which is the good news; the bad news is that most of MGC's weekly reversion bleed happens between these runs in the grind, not inside them.

Two honest caveats the raw "24 caught" hides. First, catching a run isn't capturing it: on 07-10 11:59 eight sims were correctly long into a +13.6 pop and still handed back −$88 real, chopped out before the move or stopped on the wobble. Second, the fights are ugly and repetitive — the same fade names (pullback_loose, orb_shadow, momentum_shadow firing short) kept stepping in front of gold: −$46 short into the 01:28 pop, −$41 long into the 13:14 drop, twin −$37s into the 01:35 rip. It's exactly the "every fade walked into the same gold move and got run over" pattern.

For context on who does the catching, over the full week on MGC the momentum-shaped sims are the only ones green on honest money — tw_mgc_coil_break_long +$137, tw_mgc_momo_thrust +$127, orb_shadow +$78, pullback_quiet +$47 — while the fade book bleeds hard: passive_chop −$1,125, passive_mgc_hours −$542, pullback_loose −$351, dip_loose −$293.

§4.3 3 · The footprint, clustered — what did the 10 minutes before look like?

For each cluster I took the median funnel reading at the run start, split by whether we caught it or sat it out. The question that matters: is there a cluster we keep sitting out that has a clean, separable pre-run signature — because that's the one worth building an entry for.

VACUUM — the biggest cluster, and the most frustrating. The 15 we sat out look statistically identical to the 12 we caught: atr% 0.071 vs 0.076, pre-amp 0.125 vs 0.124. There is no pre-run tell separating the vacuum runs we missed from the ones we hit. Worse, by definition these move against the tape — the 60s aggressor flow points the wrong way (e.g. 07-06 00:27, tape −110 into a +13.6 up-run). So the one microstructure signal we have actively lies about direction here. Plainly: VACUUM runs are not forecastable from anything we currently capture.

OPEN / NEWS — the clean one. The nine we sat out carry the hottest pre-run signature of any cluster: the highest atr% (0.081), a lively rvol (0.50), and the biggest pre-run amplitude (0.202, versus ~0.125 for the vacuum runs). And the signature is dead simple to detect because it's a clock: they all cluster in the 13:00–15:00 UTC US-cash-open / 10:00-ET-data window. We know when volatility is about to arrive; we just haven't been firing into it. The catch is direction — these split up and down (07-08 14:29 −24.7, 07-10 14:34 +16.3), so this is a "stand ready and go with the break," not a "predict the way" edge.

FLOW-LED — cleanest direction, thinnest evidence. Only two runs all week, both sat out, and both had strong aligned tape building before the move (07-07 19:20 up-run with tape +92, 07-07 02:37 down-run with tape −70). When the aggressor tape genuinely leads, it points the right way — but n=2 is a rumour, not a finding.

§4.4 4 · The L2 order book — could we see the vacuum coming?

Eight of the runs fall inside the MGC depth window (Thu 07-09 14:43 → Fri 07-10 21:00 UTC, ~189k snapshots). For each I looked at the 90 seconds before: did the far side of the book — the side price was about to run into — thin out ahead of time (a vacuum you could see forming), or did imbalance lean the way the run went?

rundirmoveimbalance (med)far side thinning?imbalance lean

The honest answer is no clean tell. On every one of the eight, the far side did not vacate ahead of the move — the book five-deep held roughly the same size right up to the run (before the 13:14 drop the bid stack sat at ~20 lots the whole 90s and simply got eaten — absorption, not vacuum). Imbalance leaned with the run three times, against once, and was flat the other four — a coin-flip. And the raw sizes are the real problem: MGC's top of book is 1–4 lots, five-deep only ~15–25, and imbalance flickers ±0.3 every few hundred milliseconds. There's a faint hint that the two clean down-news runs (01:30 at imbalance −0.24, 14:19 at −0.12) carried a persistently ask-heavy book beforehand, but at this size and noise it is not something I'd fire a trade on.

L2 VERDICT — MGC

Too thin to trade off. The gold book this window shows absorption, not a visible vacuum — the far side gets eaten in place rather than pulling away first, so the "see the run coming in the ladder" idea doesn't hold on MGC the way it might on the deeper MNQ book. Keep capturing; don't build an MGC L2 trigger yet.

§4.5 5 · The honest money left on the table

Add up every run and the hindsight ceiling — a perfect one-contract fill from run-start to run-end — is about $9,060. We were aligned on runs worth roughly $3,670 of that, and the sat-out runs account for $5,390. But that ceiling is a fantasy number — no strategy fills the exact top and bottom. The capturable figure — what an actual sim shape banked when it was pointed the right way — was +$756 gross, dragged to about +$483 net once you subtract the −$273 the wrong-way fills bled during those same runs. So of MGC's big-run money this week, a realistic "a sim could have taken it" number is low-hundreds of dollars a contract, not thousands. The gap between $483 realised and $5,390 sat-out is mostly the VACUUM runs we can't forecast and the OPEN/NEWS runs we chose not to be ready for.

§4.6 6 · How we grab them from Monday

OPEN / NEWS window — the one arm-able idea. shadow-first We sit out 9 of 12 of these and they carry the hottest, most predictable pre-run signature (a clock plus hot vol). Concrete trigger: between 13:00 and 15:00 UTC, when atr% > 0.08 and rvol > 0.4, and price makes a 5s break of the prior 5-minute high/low, go WITH the break — long on an up-break, short on a down-break. Direction comes from the break, not a forecast. Exit: hand it to the existing tw_mgc_coil_break chandelier, the sim already banking +$137 real on MGC. The build is small: tw_mgc_coil_break_long is long-only today — add the short leg and widen its active hours to the open window, then shadow it for a couple of weeks before arming. This is the highest-value new-entry hypothesis on gold.

FLOW-LED — watch, don't build yet. shadow-first The rule would be clean — when net aggressor tape over 60s exceeds ±50 lots and price is already moving that way, join it with the coil-break exit — but two examples all week is not enough to commit a line of code to. Log it as a shadow counter and revisit when we have 8–10 instances across a few weeks.

VACUUM — genuinely unforecastable, so play defence. no entry These are the biggest bucket (27 runs) and we have nothing in the funnel, the tape or the L2 book that separates the ones we caught from the ones we missed — the tape points the wrong way and the book gets absorbed in place. To forecast them we'd need data we don't capture: full-depth iceberg / hidden-liquidity detection to spot the big passive seller before it lifts. Until then the win is entirely defensive — stop the fade sims fading into them. The 8 fought runs cost −$273 this week, and the |slope| < 0.15 veto already on the table from the reversion autopsy would have kept pullback_loose / dip_loose out of the 08:10 dump and the 13:14 drop. That's the cheapest MGC money on offer.

PLAIN TAKEAWAY — MGC

Gold ran 57 times this week; we were right on 24 and banked a net ~$483 of a realistic few-hundred-dollar capturable pot. Most of the runs are vacuum moves against the tape that we simply cannot see coming — the funnel, the tape and the thin gold book all stay quiet or point the wrong way. The one clean, repeatable thing we keep skipping is the US-open / data-window volatility burst: build it as a two-sided break-and-go on the coil-break exit and shadow it. And whatever else, quit fading gold when it's trending — the fights, not the missed runs, are the avoidable loss.

§5 The biggest runs on M2K this week — did we show up?

M2K (micro Russell, $5 a point) printed 64 big runs this week — 15-minute moves of 5.2 points or more, which is 1.5× its normal 3.5-point quarter-hour range. We showed up in the right direction on 17 of them, faded 7, and simply weren't there for 40. When a sim did line up with a run the honest money was +$285; but M2K as a whole still lost −$252 on the week, because a fader book gave it all back on the small stuff. The runs aren't the problem. Missing them — and fading into them — is.

THE ONE-LINE STORY

M2K's real money lives in the US-cash-open hour. Every one of the 23 OPEN/NEWS runs landed between 13:05 and 14:54 UTC, they carry the fattest amplitude of the week, and we sat out 13 of them — including the four biggest sit-outs on the sheet. But the open goes both ways (13 down, 10 up) and whipsaws inside the same morning, so this is a break-to-trade, never a pre-position. Our one open sim tried to pre-position three times and got stopped all three.

§5.2 2 — Did we show up? Bluntly, less than half

outcomerunsgross travel (1-lot)honest money when involved

So of ~$3,000 of gross big-run travel this week (1-lot terms), we were pointed the right way at $924 of it and took home +$285 honest. We left $1,756 of travel completely untouched. The good news buried in here: the fades were not the bloodbath you'd fear — fighting 7 runs came out roughly flat (−$1.6), because one of them (07-10 14:34) had a momentum sim riding the right way underneath. The seven fades are cheap; the forty no-shows are the story.

WHERE THE −$252 ACTUALLY COMES FROM

M2K ran 502 honestly-scored sims this week: +$769 on paper, −$252 once you use real fills. Split it and the picture is clean — the run-catching momentum family (tw_m2k_momo_loose, momentum_shadow, ORB and the open sims) made +$170 honest; everything else — the fader book — lost −$422, of which passive_chop alone was −$304. M2K doesn't have a run problem. It has a fading-the-small-chop problem, and a showing-up-for-the-big-move problem.

§5.3 3 — The footprint before a run: it erupts from quiet

I averaged the funnel over the 10 minutes before each sit-out run. The headline is uncomfortable: volume gives you nothing. The pre-run rvol is 0.66 in the quiet cluster and 0.74 in the open cluster — and the week-average rvol across every M2K cycle is 0.649. In other words, the ten minutes before a big run look exactly like any other ten minutes on volume. M2K runs don't build; they pop out of calm.

pre-run (10 min), sit-outsatr_pctrvolvwap_distbook_imbalance
OPEN / NEWS cluster (n=12)0.0880.74−0.103+0.009
quiet / unclassified (n=27)0.0550.66−0.015+0.004
week-average baseline—0.649—~0

The only thing that separates the two clusters is the clock and the amplitude. The OPEN runs sit at roughly double the pre-run atr_pct (0.088 vs 0.055), they lean a bit below VWAP going in (−0.10), and they're the highest-amplitude runs on the sheet (amp 0.19–0.86 vs a cluster of 0.05–0.15 overnight). The funnel's L1 book-imbalance is dead flat (+0.004/+0.009) for both — it does not lean the way the run goes, so it's no help as a trigger. The one cluster we repeatedly sit out with a clean, repeatable signature is the OPEN cluster: it's not the volume, it's the wall-clock and the volatility regime. That's the new-entry hypothesis.

§5.4 4 — The L2 book: nothing usable for M2K this week

Two honest problems. First, M2K's own order book barely exists in the capture — just 10,820 snapshots, a 1.8-hour sliver on Friday evening (19:10–21:00 UTC), and not one of the 64 runs falls inside it. So for M2K's own depth, there is nothing to look at.

Second, I tried the sister-index book as a proxy — Russell moves with the Nasdaq and S&P at the open, and MNQ/MES have deep books over the Thu–Fri window. It doesn't hold up:

M2K runMNQ book, ~1 min beforeverdict
07-10 13:27  ▼ −19.1 dumpbid-heavy, imbalance +0.19 (41 bid vs 28 ask, top-5)leaned the wrong way
07-10 14:34  ▲ +10.6 popask-heavy, imbalance −0.29 to −0.55 going inleaned the wrong way

Both times the visible book leaned against the move that followed — classic limit orders getting run over, not a vacuum you could see coming. And the 14:34 "up-run" was really the recovery leg of a shared MNQ/MES V-bottom (MNQ knifed 29953→29757 then bounced), so the "run" is an artifact of where the 15-min window was cut. No clean, repeatable L2 tell for M2K this week — neither its own book (absent) nor the proxy (contrarian and noisy). To say anything real here we need M2K-native depth capture running through the cash-open hour.

§5.5 5 — The honest money left on the table

The capturable-vs-hindsight gap is smaller than the gross-travel number suggests. A perfect-hindsight ceiling would have grabbed most of $3,000 of 1-lot travel; a realistic sim shape — a momentum entry that needs the break to actually start — can only ever take the middle of a run, not the tick. This week that realistic shape, when it fired, banked +$285 honest on 17 runs, and it's lumpy: four runs (13:29 Mon +$104, 17:17 Tue +$53, 13:53 Fri +$52, 13:41 Tue +$51) are basically the entire haul. The clean, addressable miss is the 13 OPEN-cluster sit-outs worth ~$700 of 1-lot travel that share a signature we can actually detect. The 27 quiet-cluster sit-outs are mostly overnight, tiny-amplitude, and tape-blind — realistically un-catchable with what we capture, and not worth chasing.

§5.6 6 — How we grab them from Monday

§5.7 The OPEN-hour break shadow-first

The cluster: 23 runs, all between 13:05 and 14:54 UTC (the 09:30 ET cash open, ±90 min). Highest amplitude of the week. We sat out 13, including the four biggest sit-outs on the whole sheet (07-10 −19.1, 07-09 +13.9, 07-08 −13.5, 07-09 +13.0).

The trigger (observable, all from what we already capture): time is inside 13:20–15:00 UTC and atr_pct ≥ 0.09 (the open-cluster runs sit at 0.088–0.86; the quiet ones at 0.055) and price breaks the high/low of the 13:15–13:30 opening range. Direction = the direction of the break, never pre-chosen — the open was 13 down / 10 up and flips inside the same morning (Monday alone went DN, UP, DN, UP, UP), which is exactly why pre-positioning dies.

The exit: reuse the momentum_shadow / tw_m2k_momo_loose exit spine that already earned +$170 honest on M2K this week — tuned chandelier trail, not a fixed target, so a 20-pt open run isn't clipped at 2R.

Why shadow-first, not armed: our existing tw_m2k_open_momentum is the cautionary tale — it fired just 3 times this week, each a pre-positioned guess (short at 14:00 Mon into a rising tape, short 11:57 Tue, long 13:51 Fri), and all three hit their stop for −$76. The fix is the break-confirmation gate above. One week, in-sample, 23 runs — run it as an observe-only shadow with an ATR-floor and an opening-range break for 2–3 weeks before it earns a live lot.

§5.8 The quiet overnight cluster can't forecast — don't try

41 runs, spread across every hour, no tape signature, pre-run volume at the week average. M2K has no aggressor tape captured at all and its L2 book covers none of these runs. There is nothing in what we record that leads these moves — rvol doesn't build, the book doesn't lean, VWAP is flat. Chasing them now would just be curve-fitting noise. The data we'd need first: M2K-native trade_tick (aggressor flow) and depth_snap running around the clock. Until we capture M2K's own microstructure, this cluster stays a sit-out and that's the correct call.

M2K — THE PLAIN TAKEAWAY

M2K's real money is in the US-open hour, we're mostly not there, and the one sim that tries pre-positions the wrong way and gets stopped. Build a shadow-first opening-range break — only inside 13:20–15:00 UTC, only when atr_pct ≥ 0.09, trade the direction of the break, exit on the momentum chandelier. Leave the overnight quiet cluster alone until we capture M2K's own tape and book. And separately: the −$252 on the week isn't the runs at all — it's passive_chop fading small moves for −$304. Killing that does more for M2K than any new entry.

§6 The biggest runs on MYM — did we show up?

I pulled the full run census for MYM this week — every 15-minute stretch where the Dow micro travelled at least 1.5× its typical range (a move of 60 points or more, $30+ per contract). Not a top-8: the scorecard found 73 of them between Monday 00:02 and Friday 16:38 UTC. The blunt headline is that MYM ran hard and mostly ran without us: we put a sim on the right side of 11 of the 73 and sat out 62. On the 11 we did catch, the run-direction sims made +$195 of honest money — proof the shape works when it fires — but the book as a whole still bled −$559 because the fade sims kept stepping in front of the same moves. One cluster stands out as the highest-value thing to build: the US-open sell-off, which telegraphs itself in the funnel and which we sat out 19 times out of 24.

Bottom line

73 big runs, ~$3,718 of hindsight range on the table ($0.50/pt × 7,436 points). We caught 11, sat out 62, and never fought — wait, we did fight: the whole shadow book netted −$559 real (184 wins / 317 scored fills), and the single biggest bleeder is passive_chop at −$269, fading straight into these runs. The runs we joined in their direction paid +$195 — that is the capturable core. The clean, repeatable, sat-out signature is the US cash-open down-drive (16 down-runs; pre-run VWAP slope steepening to −0.36 ATR, ATR% doubling to ~0.055, net-sell order flow). MYM has no L2 book and no aggressor tape of its own this week, so the trigger has to come from the L1 funnel — and it is already sitting there.

The only strategy pointed at the runs that finished green is tw_mym_open_thrust (+$28, 7/12) — and it is pointed straight at the cluster below.

§6.3 3  The footprint, clustered — what the 10 minutes before looked like

For each cluster I averaged the L1 funnel features over the 10 minutes before each run started (fut_signal_funnel, MYM). This is the tell we would actually see in real time.

ClusterRunsatr%rvolVWAP slopeOFIPlain footprint
US open — DN (16)160.0560.79−0.36−5.5ATR doubles, VWAP rolls over hard, sellers lean
US open — UP (8)80.0510.71+0.05−12.2V-snap reversals; slope does NOT pre-lean up
Quiet drift — UP (29)290.0260.61+0.31−6.6slow grind, tiny amplitude
Quiet drift — DN (20)200.0270.62−0.42+5.6slow grind, tiny amplitude

Two things jump out. First, the US-open down cluster has a clean, one-directional pre-run signature: in the 10 minutes before the sell-off, ATR% is already running at ~0.056 (double the quiet-day 0.026), the VWAP slope is steeply negative (−0.36 ATR), and order-flow imbalance leans net-sell. I checked how reliable that slope tell is: 10 of the 16 open down-runs had a pre-run VWAP slope steeper than −0.10 — price was already rolling before the leg. That is a footprint you can trade off.

Second, the US-open UP runs do NOT telegraph: their pre-run slope is a flat +0.05 and only 2 of 8 pre-leaned up — because they are V-bottom reversals (Friday's +232 came the minute after a −203 flush). And the quiet-drift clusters, while their slope aligns with direction, run at a third of the amplitude (0.026 ATR%, 60–80-point moves worth $30–40) — forecastable-ish but far too small and far too many to trade without drowning in chop tax. That quiet bucket is exactly where passive_chop lost its $269.

The one to build

The highest-value sat-out signature is unambiguous: the US cash-open down-drive. Sixteen of them, we sat out most, and every one arrives on a pre-run footprint of expanding ATR + steep negative VWAP slope + net-sell flow that we already capture in the funnel.

§6.4 4  The L2 book — MYM has none, so I used MNQ as a proxy

Straight answer: MYM is not in the depth capture and has no aggressor tape this week — depth.db covers MNQ/MES/MGC only, and trade_tick covers MNQ/MES/MGC/MBT. So there is no native L2 tell for the Dow micro. The nearest thing is to read the MNQ book as a correlated index proxy over the two Friday runs that fall inside the depth window (Thu 14:17 → Fri 21:00 UTC): the −203 down-flush at 14:18 and the +232 V-snap at 14:33.

In the 90 seconds before MYM's −203, the MNQ book imbalance averaged −0.044 (a faint ask-heavy, down lean) across 358 snaps — but it swung from −0.51 to +0.35 tick-to-tick. Before the +232, it averaged +0.045 (faint bid lean) across 339 snaps, swinging −0.48 to +0.93. So the mean leans the right way by a whisker each time, but the sign flips several times a second and the top-of-book sizes are tiny (1–11 lots). I looked for a far-side vacuum — the ask stack thinning out 1–2 minutes ahead — and did not find a clean, repeatable one; the book just churns.

read Honest verdict on L2 for MYM: no usable book tell. Even on the proxy the signal is buried in noise, and MYM has no book of its own anyway. If the owner wants a genuine L2 edge on the Dow micro, the data we would need is MYM depth capture (it is not entitled/subscribed today) — until then the funnel's L1 slope + OFI is the best pre-run read we have.

§6.5 5  The honest money left on the table

The hindsight ceiling — catching every one of the 73 runs top-to-bottom — is ~$3,718 per contract (7,436 points × $0.50). That is a fantasy number; nobody catches the full range of a 15-minute leg. The capturable figure is what a real sim shape actually banked when it joined a run in its direction: +$195 across the 11 we caught. The gap between −$559 (what the book really did) and +$195 (what the run-joins did) is −$754 of self-inflicted damage — almost all of it passive_chop and the stand-down gates fading into the moves. So the money left on the table isn't really the runs we missed; it's the fades we should never have taken. Stop the fade bleed and the same book is already positive on MYM before we add a single new entry.

Chart — every run this week, by size ($), in time order. Green bars = we caught it (sim fired with the run); red = we sat out. Gold ticks along the bottom mark the US-open/news cluster. The eye-test is the point of the section: a long red wall of sell-offs (the 08:15 −$154, the 13:26 −$118, the 14:33 +$116) with only a scatter of green — and almost every gold-ticked big bar is red.

§6.6 6  How we grab them from Monday

One cluster is worth arming, one is a stand-down, and one needs data we don't have.

Prescription A — the US-open down-drive (arm as shadow, ready to promote)

When: in the 13:30–15:00 UTC cash-open window, and atr_pct ≥ 0.05 (amplitude is live, not a quiet day) and vwap_slope_atr ≤ −0.15 (price already rolling under VWAP) and vwap_dist_pct < 0 (trading below value) and OFI ≤ 0 (sellers leaning). Direction: SHORT, with the drive — do NOT fade it. Exit: the tw_mym_open_thrust shape is the proven +EV catcher here (+$28, 7/12) — extend it to the short side, ride with a tuned chandelier / give-back trail and a hard 0.75×ATR stop, flat by the 15:00 window close or 20-min clock, whichever first. This mirrors what the capture-lab found independently (open-only momentum is the one net-positive construction). Status: shadow-first — the trigger is in-sample on one week and thin-n; run it observe-only next week and promote if it holds. It fires ~16 times/week and 10/16 pre-leaned correctly, so it is armable-soon, not armable-blind-today.

Prescription B — the quiet-drift bucket (stand DOWN)

Forty-nine runs, but they run at 0.026 ATR% and pay $30–40 apiece. The slope mildly aligns, so they are forecastable, but trading them means many small entries in dead tape — precisely the tape where passive_chop lost $269 this week. The action here is subtractive: stop passive_chop and the two stand-down gates from firing in this window. That recovers more than any new entry would add. No new long/short to build.

Prescription C — the US-open UP snaps (can't forecast from what we capture)

The eight open UP runs are V-bottom reversals (Friday's +232 the minute after a −203). Their pre-run slope is flat +0.05 and OFI is actually net-sell just before they rip — contrarian, un-leadable from L1. To catch these we would need a genuine reversal trigger (a fast absorption/exhaustion read), and that means MYM-native L2 depth + aggressor tape, which we do not have. Name it and park it: no honest entry from current data.

Plain MYM takeaway: MYM ran 73 times this week and we joined 11 — and the joins made money (+$195) while the fades lost it all back and more (book −$559, passive_chop −$269). There is exactly one clean, repeatable, sat-out edge: short the US cash-open sell-off when ATR is expanding, VWAP is rolling over, and flow leans sell — with the open-thrust exit that already prints green. Arm it shadow-first, and in the same breath stop the chop-fades that are the real leak. Everything else on MYM this week is either too small to bother with or needs a book we don't yet capture.

§7 The biggest runs on MCL this week — did we show up?

Crude ran plenty this week. The scorecard counts 78 separate 15-minute moves of $40 or more on MCL between Monday 07-06 and Friday 07-10 — every one at least 1.5× the contract's typical 15-minute range (that typical range is only about $30, so crude is a jumpy little contract). We sat out 50 of the 78. When we did show up, the honest money was a wash: for every momentum win there was a fade that got run over. The real bleed on MCL isn't the runs we miss — it's the chop we trade between them.

READ THIS FIRST — WE ARE BLIND ON MCL

Before anything else: MCL has no order book and effectively no tape. The L2 depth farm (depth.db) only carries MNQ, MES, MGC and M2K — NYMEX crude came back "not subscribed" (Err354). The aggressor tape has just 2,047 MCL ticks all week versus 7.4 million for MNQ. So the flow column in the census is blank, Section 4's book work is impossible here, and every read below leans on 5-second bars and the signal funnel alone. That is the single biggest thing holding MCL back.

§7.2 2 · Did we show up? Bluntly, mostly no

Across the whole 78-run census we fired the right way on 28 and sat out 50. Broken down by cause:

ClusterRunsSat outCaughtReal $ when with-run
Vol-expansion break28217+109
Quiet drift (unclassified)341915+114
Open / news spike16106+220

The honest money picture, once and for all. Taking every MCL sim that fired with a run within five minutes: 38 trades, +$443 real. Every sim that fired against a run (a fade walking into the move): 41 trades, −$414 real. So around the runs themselves we net roughly flat — about +$29. But the desk's total on MCL for the week was −$1,716 across 690 trades. That means essentially the entire loss came from grinding chop between the runs, not from the runs. The two worst offenders: passive_chop long+short (−$700 real) and tw_mcl_short_with_strength (295 trades, a rosy +$958 in the optimistic column but −$341 once you price the real fills — a $1,300 optimism gap, the classic reversion-fading-into-a-grind).

THE ONE THING THAT WORKS ON MCL

momentum_shadow LONG: 36 trades, +$152.68 real, 19 wins / 17 losses, best trade +$52.88, worst just −$16.12, average hold about 30 minutes. That asymmetry — small capped losers, winners left to run — is the entire edge. It is the only MCL shape in the black, and it is the shape that catches crude's runs.

§7.3 3 · The footprint before each cluster

What did the ten minutes before each type of run look like in the funnel (we have no tape or book here, remember)?

ClusterPre-run atr%Pre-run rvolVWAP dist%VWAP slope (10m)Plain read
Vol-expansion0.1540.75+0.119+0.037range already widening, price already leaning the break way
Open / news0.1520.63+0.031+0.02713:00–14:30 UTC pit / inventory prints; violent, two-sided
Quiet drift0.1100.59−0.015−0.014low amplitude, no build — genuinely low signal

The cleanest, most repeated miss is the vol-expansion break. We sat out 21 of 28 of them — three quarters — and they carry a legible fingerprint: atr_pct already climbing up through about 0.15 (well above the quiet-drift 0.11) and VWAP distance already positive by +0.12 with a gentle slope the direction the break then goes. In plain words: the range starts widening and price is already leaning before the leg fires. That is a momentum-continuation footprint, not a mystery. The morning of 07-08 is the textbook case — the $230 whale at 08:11 followed by $80 pushes at 08:27 and 08:48, then more at 12:04 and 12:54, all vol-expansion, all sat out, one clean up-trend we watched from the bench.

§7.4 4 · The L2 book — nothing to show, and that's the point

NO MCL BOOK EXISTS

The depth window (Thu 07-09 14:17 → Fri 07-10 21:00 UTC) covers MNQ, MES, MGC and M2K. MCL is not in depth.db at all — zero snapshots — because the NYMEX L2 entitlement returns "not subscribed". Fourteen MCL runs fall inside that window (07-09 14:49, 07-10 07:10 through 15:53, and so on) and for not one of them can we say whether the far side thinned out ahead of the move or got eaten, or whether book imbalance leaned the right way. There is no book tell to report because there is no book. This is not "too noisy" — it is missing data, and it is the top of the MCL wishlist: a NYMEX depth entitlement so crude gets the same vacuum-vs-absorption read we now have on MNQ/MES/MGC.

§7.5 5 · The honest money left on the table

Add up the dollar size of all 78 runs and it is a large hindsight ceiling — but most of it is not capturable by any sim shape we own, because a $40 15-minute wiggle is not a tradable leg once you price the crude spread and slippage. The capturable money is the subset with a real footprint and a real exit that holds: the vol-expansion and open/news legs. When momentum_shadow did catch one it was clean — +$100.76 on the 07-08 14:13 leg, +$82.64 on 07-10 13:34, +$47.76 on 07-09 08:53. Extrapolating the +$443 we banked on 38 with-run fires to the ~50 similar runs we sat out suggests order $400–$600 of realistically-capturable run money left on the table this week — but that is one week, in-sample, and it comes bundled with the fade losses you have to stop first.

§7.6 6 · How we grab them from Monday

Vol-expansion break — the highest-value miss (21/28 sat out). SHADOW-FIRST

Trigger, stated concretely: when MCL atr_pct crosses up through ~0.13–0.15 and VWAP distance is already positive in the break direction (rvol around 0.7–0.8), go with the break — long on an up-lean, short on a down-lean. Exit: the existing momentum_shadow chandelier that holds ~30 minutes and lets the winner run. We already own the entry and the exit; momentum_shadow is the only +EV MCL shape. The gap is coverage — it fired on just 6 of 28 vol-expansion runs. The action is to widen momentum_shadow's amplitude/lean gate on MCL and watch it in shadow for a week or two before arming. Shadow-first, not arm-now.

Open / news spike — best dollars when caught, but violent (10/16 sat out). SHADOW-FIRST, CAUTIOUS

Trigger: inside the 13:00–14:30 UTC US pit / inventory window, with atr_pct around 0.15, take the first clean break direction with the same momentum_shadow exit. This cluster paid the most per fire (+$220 real across 5). But it is genuinely two-sided event risk — the 07-10 14:33 print whipped 2.7× range in both directions — and MCL's crude spread eats thin edges. Arm only after a shadow soak, and honestly accept half of these are news coin-flips we cannot forecast from bars alone.

Quiet drift — do not chase, and stop fading. KILL THE FADES

These 34 low-amplitude drifts (atr% 0.11, no build) carry no forecastable signature — sitting most of them out is correct. The prescription here is not a new entry, it is subtraction: stop the reversion fades that bleed between the runs. passive_chop and tw_mcl_short_with_strength together cost ~$1,000 of real shadow money this week fading into moves; on MCL specifically they should stand down. That, not a new entry, is the biggest single MCL improvement available.

THE PLAIN TAKEAWAY — MCL

We are flying blind on crude — no book, almost no tape — and it shows: we sat out 50 of 78 real runs, including the $230 whale. But the runs are not where we lose money; around them we net about flat. The −$1,716 came from fading the chop in between. Two moves for Monday: (1) widen momentum_shadow (the one +EV MCL shape) to cover vol-expansion breaks, shadow-first; (2) stand the reversion fades down on MCL. And put a NYMEX depth entitlement on the wishlist — until crude has a book, everything here is half-sighted.

§8 The biggest runs on MBT this week — did we show up?

Micro-Bitcoin threw off 107 big runs this week — 15-minute lurches of 232 points or more, which for MBT at $0.10 a point is roughly $23+ of range in a quarter of an hour. We fired a sim in the right direction on just 19 of them (18%), sat out the other 88, and still managed to lose $2,048 of honest money in the attempt. The runs are real, they are large, and they cluster at a time of day we can see coming a mile off. We just keep showing up in the wrong session with the wrong shape.

READ THIS FIRST — TWO DATA CAVEATS FOR MBT

MBT is not in the L2 order book (depth.db only carries MNQ / MES / MGC / M2K), so Section 4 has no book to show you — I use the aggressor tape instead and tell you plainly it doesn't lead. And MBT was the contract dropped from the tape booster (6 names kept, MBT cut), so its aggressor feed is thin — 86k ticks over six days vs the dense MNQ tape. Treat every "flow" number here as low-confidence. The one thing MBT has in spades is a clean, dense 5s bar record, so the run census itself is solid.

§8.2 2 · Participation — we caught 19, and lost the money anyway

Here is the blunt version. Of 107 runs we fired in-direction on 19 (18%) and sat out 88. But firing is not the same as making money. Across 705 MBT sim trades this week (511 filled), the honest tally is −$2,047.75. The optimistic sim number was −$1,234.92, so even the doomed entries we took were understated by the sim by about 1.7×, exactly as the desk keeps finding. We are not sitting out because we're being disciplined — we're firing plenty, we're just firing the wrong shapes.

Ten of twelve shapes bled. The two that made anything — orb_shadow (+$21.50) and tw_us_open_surge_cont (+$11.00) — are both opening-break continuation shapes. And the single worst line, passive_chop at −$860, is a fade — it sells strength and buys weakness into a market that this week simply kept going. That is the whole chapter in two rows: fading MBT lost, going with the break made a little. Momentum_shadow (−$37 on 75, 31 wins) is the honourable near-miss — the right instinct, poorly exited.

§8.3 3 · The footprint — when MBT runs, and why we're in the wrong session

The single loudest signal in this whole chapter is a clock. Sort every run by hour of day and MBT lights up like a switchboard at the US cash open — 29 of 107 runs land in the 13:00–15:59 UTC window, and the OPEN/NEWS cluster is entirely in the 13:00 and 14:00 hours (11 runs at 13:00, 10 at 14:00). Bitcoin trades the S&P's open.

MBT big runs by UTC hour, in-week. Gold = the 13:00–15:00 US-open window (29 runs). Leftmost bar = 00:00 UTC, rightmost = 23:00.

Now overlay where the money went. Split the sim P&L by session and the picture is upside down:

Session (UTC)Sim tradesHonest P&LRead
London 05:00–12:59449-$1,161.80Where we over-trade — fading quiet drifts
US-pm 17:00–21:5940-$388.50Thin, still bleeding
Asia 22:00–04:59131-$268.50Overnight chop fades
US-open 13:00–16:5985-$229.00The best hours — and we barely trade them
THE MAP IS BACKWARDS

We do five times the trading in the quiet London window (449 trades, −$1,162) as we do in the US-open window (85 trades, −$229), yet London is where the fades get run over and the US open is where the only green shapes live. We're over-trading the dead session and under-trading the alive one.

Reading the pre-run footprint by cluster: the UNCLASSIFIED drifts (48 runs, amp 0.160) have no signature you can trade — the tape is asleep and price just walks, which is precisely what mauls passive_chop. The VOL-EXPANSION breaks (10 runs, amp 0.577) announce themselves — ATR is already screaming — but you're late by the time you see it. The OPEN/NEWS cluster is the sweet spot: 21 runs, the biggest average move (403pt), amp already firming to 0.354, and — crucially — a clock you can set. That is the highest-value thing we repeatedly sit out with a clean, forecastable signature.

§8.4 4 · The book — MBT has none, and the tape doesn't lead

The brief asks for the L2 read on the runs inside the depth window. For MBT the honest answer is short: there is no L2 book for MBT. The depth capture covers MNQ, MES, MGC and M2K only — MBT was never entitled or booked. So I can't show you a far-side vacuum or an absorption print, because we never recorded the ladder.

What MBT does have is the aggressor tape (86k prints, 07-05 → 07-10), so I checked whether the tape leads the runs. It doesn't. Three OPEN/NEWS examples, buy vs sell volume in the 10 minutes before the run:

RunDirBuy volSell volDid the tape call it?
07-06 13:38UP +650788522Weak yes — 1.5:1 buy lean
07-07 14:44UP +7001,005973No — dead balanced before a 700pt rip
07-09 13:53DN -510540274Backwards — buy-heavy right before a −510 drop

That is why the scorecard tags so many MBT runs as VACUUM ("moved against the tape") — the thin MBT tape genuinely does not lead the move, and on the biggest bursts it's either flat or pointing the wrong way. So for MBT, do not build a flow-triggered entry — the tape we have isn't good enough and there's no book to lean on. The signature that works is the clock and the amplitude, not the order flow. If we wanted a real book tell on MBT we'd need to entitle and capture the CME Bitcoin depth feed — flag that as the data gap, not a this-week edge.

§8.5 5 · The honest money left on the table

Add up the raw magnitude of all 107 runs and you get 38,805 points of directional travel this week. At MBT's $0.10 a point that is $3,880 of hindsight range on a single micro contract — the theoretical ceiling if you bought every low and sold every high. Nobody captures that; it's the fantasy number. Set it against reality: the sims that actually traded lost $2,048, and even their best-case exits (the ceiling on the trades we took) came to −$1,235. So the gap isn't "we captured 60% and could get to 80%" — it's that we're on the wrong side of the ledger entirely, converting a market full of big clean moves into a four-figure loss. The capturable money is real and large; the shapes pointed at it are not.

§8.6 6 · How we grab them from Monday

One cluster, the OPEN/NEWS burst, is both the biggest and the only one with a signature we can act on ahead of time. Here is the concrete plan, plus the housekeeping that stops the bleed.

§8.7 Lead — the US-open continuation break SHADOW-FIRST

When: the 13:00–15:00 UTC window only (US cash open). Trigger: MBT amp/ATR firming through ~0.30 and price breaking the 13:00–13:30 opening range in the break direction. Direction: with the break — long the up-break, short the down-break, no fading. Exit: the RATCHET/chandelier that already carries tw_us_open_surge_cont's two winners (07-09 13:51 LONG +$28.5, 07-08 14:52 LONG +$16, both closed on RATCHET) — let it run and trail, don't cap it at a fixed R. Why shadow-first, not armed: the two green shapes are real but thin — orb_shadow's usable +$100 came almost entirely from one day (07-06, riding the +1130 monster), and us_open_surge_cont is +$11 on 6 trades. This is one in-sample week — promising, not proven. Widen tw_us_open_surge_cont's window to the full 13:00–15:00 band and observe another 2–3 weeks before it earns a live nomination.

STAND DOWN — stop feeding the London fades

The −$1,162 London-session loss is almost all passive_chop and the other fades selling into quiet UNCLASSIFIED drifts that keep walking. Disarm passive_chop, tw_mbt_fade_euphoria and pullback_quiet on MBT outright — MBT is a trending, open-driven crypto contract, not a mean-reverter, and every fade shape confirmed that again this week. This doesn't make money (it's shadow), it just stops us manufacturing a −$860 line item. That's a "trade less," and on MBT trading less is the correct move.

§8.8 Watch, don't build — the other three clusters

VOL-EXPANSION (amp 0.577): the move is already underway when the signature appears, so an entry is late — park it. VACUUM & UNCLASSIFIED: these are the quiet air-pocket drifts with no forecastable pre-run tell in anything we capture — no book, weak tape. Genuinely unforecastable from current data. The only thing that would change that is a real MBT L2 depth feed to watch the far side thin out; until we entitle it, don't pretend there's an entry here.

Takeaway for MBT: the market handed us 107 big runs and $3,880 of hindsight range on one micro, and we turned it into a −$2,048 loss by fading quiet London drifts we should never touch while barely trading the 13:00–15:00 US-open bursts where the only green shapes live. Monday: disarm the MBT fades, and shadow-widen the US-open continuation break with a ratchet exit. Don't build a flow trigger — MBT has no book and its tape doesn't lead.

§9 How We Grab The Runs From Monday

This is the capstone you asked for — not another contract-by-contract lab, but the one desk-wide answer to “where was the money this week, did we touch it, and exactly how do we get involved on Monday?” I ran the full run census (footprint_scorecard.py missed --top 100) on all seven contracts, tagged every big move by its cause, and joined the honest money (real_pnl, never the optimistic sim figure). The short version: the runs are real, plentiful, and mostly catchable — and we already have one family of sims that catches them and prints green. The bleed isn’t that we can’t find the runs; it’s that the fade/stand-down sims spent the week fighting them.

Bottom line

Across all seven contracts this week there were 540 runs of ≥1.5× typical range. We were involved (any sim fired the right way) in just 127 of them and sat out 413 — three-quarters of the money in motion. Yet the honest scoreboard says the direction is right: the with-the-run family made +$2,441 real this week (led by tw_mnq_thrust_cont at +$992, 28 trades, 61% win, +$35/fire — and its real P&L beat its optimistic sim, so it’s honest), while the whole desk netted −$8,599 because the fades and stand-downs gave back −$11,040 fighting the same moves. The plan below is one sentence: stop fading the runs, and extend the confirmed-momentum entry to the clean-flow runs we keep sitting out. All figures USD; GBP ≈ USD×0.79.

§9.3 3  The Monday capture play for each cause cluster

For each cluster: the tell you can actually observe at entry, the direction, the exit shape (which existing sim takes it), and arm-now versus shadow-first. Ranked by sat-out money addressed × how clean the signal is.

ARM NOW 1. FLOW-LED runs (aggressors drove it) — 35 runs, 28 sat out, and the only cluster with a clean, forward-observable tell. Trigger: one-sided aggressor tape (trade_tick buy/sell imbalance strongly in the run direction over the last 30–60s) confirmed by the L2 book leaning the same way (depth_snap.imbalance same sign) and a real thrust (atr_pct rising, rvol ≥ 1.5). Direction: WITH the flow. Exit: the confirmed-momentum + chandelier shape that already works — i.e. tw_mnq_thrust_cont. Where: MNQ (proven, deep tape + 360k book snaps) and MES (deep tape + book). This is the highest-value play because it stacks the most sat-out runs that carry a clean signature on the contracts where we actually have the data.

SHADOW FIRST 2. OPEN / NEWS runs — 138 runs, 107 sat out, the second-biggest bucket. Tell: the timing is knowable (the 13:30–14:35 UTC US-open / data window fires nearly every day, on every contract). But the direction is a coin-flip and the tails are brutal — the 07-10 14:33 spike alone showed a −$24k optimistic swing on MES. Play: do NOT predict direction. Wait for the first 5-minute range to set, then take the break with confirming flow, small size, hard stop. Exit: ORB-style target = 1× opening-range height. Shadow-first — the edge is real in timing but unproven in direction, and one bad tail erases a month.

DO NOT BUILD AN ENTRY 3. VACUUM runs (moved AGAINST the tape) — 123 runs, 92 sat out. These are the second-biggest bucket and the most seductive, but the name is the verdict: price ran while the aggressor tape pointed the other way (stop-runs, liquidity air-pockets). Any flow-based entry would have been pointed backwards into them. This is precisely where the fade book got run over. The lesson is defensive, not offensive: these are un-tradeable from flow — the value is telling the reversion sims to stop fading into them.

CAN’T — DATA GAP 4. UNCLASSIFIED runs (no tape / quiet) — 209 runs, 160 sat out, the biggest bucket of all. Sounds like the jackpot; it isn’t. 183 of those 209 are on M2K, MYM, MCL and MBT — the four contracts where we have essentially no aggressor tape and no L2 book (M2K 4.9k ticks, MCL 2.0k, MYM 5.3k, and no book on any). “Unclassified” here means “the classifier is blind because we never captured the data,” not “a quiet edge waiting.” On the three tape-covered contracts unclassified collapses to just 26 runs. This bucket is a capture-coverage problem, not a strategy.

A flow-led thrust entry on MNQ and MES. The confirmed-momentum + chandelier machinery from tw_mnq_thrust_cont (already +$992 real, honest, 61% win) is the exit; the only new part is the entry gate: fire WITH the run when (a) aggressor tape is one-sided in the run direction over the last 30–60s, (b) the L2 book imbalance leans the same way, and (c) rvol ≥ 1.5 with atr_pct expanding. MNQ has the deepest tape and 360k book snaps to prove it; MES is the natural second. Everything else on this page is either already working (lean harder), a coin-flip that needs a cautious shadow (open-news), a trap to stop fading (vacuum), or blocked until we capture the tape (M2K/MYM/MCL).

§9.6 6  Monday action list

3. STOP THE BLEED Cut the reversion / stand-down sims that fade into vacuum runs (passive_chop, tw_deadtape_standdown, tw_clock_standdown, tw_ride_strength) — they are the entire −$11k. Fading the runs is the loss.

Takeaway: the runs are there and we already know how to ride one — the with-the-run sims made +$2,441 this week and tw_mnq_thrust_cont is honest green. Monday isn’t about finding a new edge; it’s about pointing the proven confirmed-momentum entry at the clean-flow runs we keep sitting out, and telling the fade book to stop walking into the vacuum. Everything past that is either a cautious shadow or a data-capture job — and one week of in-sample runs means these are hypotheses to shadow, not truths to size.

§10 Momentum is confirmed-vs-loose, one contract at a time

Read this first — momentum is TWO-SIDED, no fading
Before anything below is misread: a momentum gate trades both ways — it longs a rising thrust and shorts a falling one — and it stays that way. Never long-only. This week the confirmed-momentum short side made +$1,435, more than its long side (+$1,006); on MES and MGC the short side was the only green side. What we kill is fading (fighting a live move), not shorting. Don't let "confirmed vs loose" get quietly turned into "long vs short."

The momentum family split cleanly down the middle this week, and it split by mechanism, not by name. The sims that make you wait for the move to be confirmed — a real volume surge, a high-participation rvol gate, a trend-regime that agrees — banked +$2,344 across 909 shadow fills. The sims that just ride any strength — a 0.30-ATR blip, a down-flush, an open thrust with nothing behind it — bled −$1,017 across 509. Same idea, "go with the move", opposite outcome. The difference is the confirmation.

THE BARBELL

Both wings win under 45% of the time — momentum is a low-win, let-the-winner-run game whichever way you cut it. The confirmed wing survives that because its winners are nearly twice its losers (avg win +$26.15 vs avg loss −$13.75, ~1.9:1). The loose wing does not: its winners only run ~1.35:1 (+$18.79 vs −$13.97) and it wins less often (36.5% vs 40.9%), so it drifts red. Confirmation barely lifts the hit-rate — what it does is make the winners bigger and the losers rarer.

§10.1 The per-contract matrix — where momentum actually pays

Broken out by contract on honest real_pnl, the edge isn't "momentum works". It's "confirmed momentum works on the index-and-gold micros, and nowhere else." MNQ is the engine of the entire number; MGC, M2K and MES chip in positive; crude, bitcoin and the thin Dow micro don't reward it even when you confirm.
